# Glasp: history

Reviewed **2026-10-19 01:44, Europe/Warsaw**. Repository source **122**: [category](https://glasp.co/books/category/history).

The saved category snapshot contains **157 listed records**. This report accounts for **157 distinct works**: 132 eligible, 20 vetoed, 4 hard-excluded and 1 non-book references.

The complete rendered category was retrieved through a public reader after direct access failed. The listed-record count matches the page total. The captured page exposes no comment thread or pagination controls; live browser expansion could not be verified. [Saved source](data/glasp_261019/history.txt) · [Full inventory](data/glasp_261019/inventory.json). Titles come only from the supplied source pages; publisher, library and critical sources establish identity, purpose and assessment evidence. Publisher promotion is not independent proof of quality.

## Method and reading priority

Fiction and primarily instructional or specialist technical works are removed before vetoes. All remaining non-vetoed works are scored, including poor fits. E, S and D use their own quadratic profiles; the other seven criteria remain linear. T11 and T12 are separate thematic deductions. H13 uses the assessed historical endpoint, P14 the original production year, and O15 one verified Amazon rating aggregate. Unknown Amazon counts receive the required zero-deduction fallback, not a claim of popularity. Calculations and sorting retain full precision; displayed values use two decimals.

Verdicts: Recommend ≥85; Consider ≥75; Selective ≥65; Low priority ≥50; Poor fit <50. Confidence describes evidence strength, not statistical probability. Reader aggregates are separate from the quality score. A missing rating is shown as unavailable; no rating is invented for Book Marks, LibraryThing, StoryGraph, Goodreads or Amazon.

The highest-ranked eligible works are *The Fabric of Civilization* (90.70), *Where Good Ideas Come From* (84.51), *The Idea Factory* (80.84), *The Making of the Atomic Bomb* (78.70), *The Silk Roads* (73.22). The lowest-ranked eligible works are *A History of Rome* (10.46), *The Autobiography of Benjamin Franklin* (8.99), *The Spectator* (3.67); low scores do not remove them from the recommendation ledger.

## Hard exclusions

No scores are assigned to these works. One underlying work can have several source-edition entries.

| Work | Rule · confidence | Reason and evidence |
|---|---|---|
| All Quiet on the Western Front — Erich Maria Remarque | 1 · high | Remarque’s war novel is fiction. Historical setting and author experience do not make it a memoir. [1](https://glasp.co/books/p/all-quiet-on-the-western-front-a-novel) [2](https://openlibrary.org/works/OL1209288W) Source entries: [All Quiet on the Western Front Erich Maria Remarque](https://glasp.co/books/p/all-quiet-on-the-western-front-a-novel). |
| Intellectual Life in the Middle Ages — Lesley M. Smith; Benedicta Ward (editors) | 2 · moderate | A specialist research festschrift whose contents center on manuscript glosses, source authorship and narrow medieval textual problems. It is aimed at specialist scholars rather than a sustained general-reader account; apply rule 2 before any topic-fit veto. [1](https://books.google.com/books/about/Intellectual_Life_in_the_Middle_Ages.html?hl=en&id=snJdsO8DlSoC&output=html_text) Source entries: [Intellectual Life in the Middle Ages Lesley M. Smith](https://glasp.co/books/p/intellectual-life-in-the-middle-ages-essays-presented-to-margaret-gibson). |
| Karl Marx's Theory of History — G. A. Cohen | 2 · moderate | An advanced analytic-philosophy monograph reconstructing and defending historical materialism through specialist conceptual techniques. The publisher identifies that technical purpose and structure. Apply the expert-monograph hard exclusion before considering an ideological veto. [1](https://www.jstor.org/stable/j.ctv105b973) Source entries: [Karl Marx's Theory of History G. A. Cohen](https://glasp.co/books/p/karl-marx-s-theory-of-history). |
| The Last Days of Night — Graham Moore | 1 · high | Graham Moore’s historical novel fictionalizes the electrical patent conflict. Real inventors and technical subject matter do not make it nonfiction. [1](https://glasp.co/books/p/the-last-days-of-night-a-novel) [2](https://openlibrary.org/works/OL20253254W) Source entries: [The Last Days of Night Graham Moore](https://glasp.co/books/p/the-last-days-of-night-a-novel). |

<a id="g1112"></a>
### The Life of Greece

**Will Durant; original production 1939. 19.03/100; Poor fit. Score confidence: low.**

Assess volume II, The Life of Greece. Correct the library work’s erroneous 1935 series date to the volume’s 1939 publication. ### The Gallic Wars

**Julius Caesar; Aulus Hirtius (book VIII); original production -50. Veto V2; counterfactual 11.33/100; not recommended. Score confidence: moderate.**

V2, moderate confidence: a public self-justification by the commander and political beneficiary of conquest. Use an approximate 50 BCE composition date; the eighth commentary has a separate author and uncertain later date, which does not affect the capped deduction.

Direct access supports A, but political selection lowers E. Logistics and command support S/U/M/F; concise organization supports D/N/P. R is high as a primary historical source, not as an impartial assessment.
